0\to{\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}}\to\rho_{*}{\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{\widetilde{X},\widetilde{P}_{\bullet},\widetilde{R}_{\bullet}}\to\bigoplus_{q\text{ R-node}}{\mathcal{O}}_{q}\to 0

where the summation is over all Ramond nodes.

(ii) One has
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}})=0
.

Proof.

(i) We have a natural morphism

\kappa:{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}}\to\rho_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{\widetilde{X},\widetilde{P}_{\bullet},\widetilde{R}_{\bullet}}

which is an isomorphism
away from the nodes. Thus, the assertion can be checked by a local computation near the nodes (so we can forget about the punctures).
Near an NS node we have

{\mathcal{A}}^{+}_{X}\simeq\rho_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{\widetilde{C}}(-q_{1}-q_{2}),\ \ {\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{X}\simeq\rho_{*}\widetilde{L}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{\widetilde{C}}(-q_{1}-q_{2}),

where
\{q_{1},q_{2}\}\subset\widetilde{C}
is the preimage of the node, so
\kappa
is an isomorphism.

Near a Ramond node we have

{\mathcal{A}}^{+}_{X}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\simeq\rho_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{\widetilde{C}}(-q_{1}-q_{2}),\ \ {\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{X}\simeq L\otimes\omega_{C}^{-1},

whereas

{\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{\widetilde{X}}\simeq\widetilde{L}\otimes\omega^{-1}_{\widetilde{C}}(-q_{1}-q_{2})\simeq\rho^{*}(L\otimes\omega_{C}^{-1}).

Thus,
\kappa^{+}
is an isomorphism, while
\kappa^{-}
is an embedding with the cokernel of length 1 supported at the node.

(ii)
Since global infinitesimal automorphisms of
X
embed into those for
\widetilde{X}
,
it is enough to prove the assertion in the case when
C
is smooth. We can also assume it is connected.

When
C
is smooth we have

{\mathcal{A}}_{X}^{+}={\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j}),\ \ {\mathcal{A}}_{X}^{-}=L\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j}).

We have
L^{2}\simeq\omega_{C}(\sum r_{j})
, so

\deg L=g-1+n_{R}/2,

\deg L\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j})=-(g-1)-n_{NS}-n_{R}/2,\ \ \deg{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j})=-2(g-1)-n_{NS}-n_{R}.

Note that
\deg{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j})<0
by stability of the underlying pointed curve.
Hence,

\deg L\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j})=\frac{1}{2}\deg{\mathcal{T}}_{C}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{j})-n_{NS}/2<0,

so
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}^{\pm}
do not have global sections.
∎

3.5.
Smooth affine supercurves without Ramond punctures

Let
X_{0}
be an affine (smooth) supercurve over
\C
.

Lemma 3.12
.

For every
\C
-point
p\in X_{0}
, and every tangent vector
v_{0}\in T_{p}X_{0}
, there exists a superconformal
vector field
v
on
X_{0}
with
v(p)=v_{0}
.

Proof.

First, we claim that there is an exact sequence of sheaves with respect to étale topology

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0},p}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}\to i_{*}T_{p}X_{0}\to 0

where
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}={\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}}^{sc}
. Indeed, for this we need to check the surjectivity of the last arrow.
Locally in étale topology the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}}
has a generator
D=\partial_{\theta}+\theta\partial_{z}
(see Lemma
2.10
).
Then the vector fields
D-2\theta D^{2}
and
D^{2}
are superconformal and restrict to a basis of
T_{p}X_{0}
.

Next, we observe that
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0},p}
is isomorphic to a coherent sheaf on
X_{0}
(see Proposition
3.9
).
Hence,
H^{1}(X_{0},{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0},p})=0
and we obtained the required surjectivity of the map
H^{0}(X_{0},{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}})\to T_{p}X_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
A\to A/I=B
be a small extension in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
,
X_{0}
a superscheme over
\C
,
E_{0}
a vector bundle on
X_{0}
,
D_{0}\subset E_{0}
a subbundle.
We denote by

(X_{B}=X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(B),E_{B}=B\otimes_{\C}E_{0},D_{B}=B\otimes_{\C}D_{0})

the trivial deformation of these data over
B
. Consider
X_{A}=X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
with the vector bundle
E_{A}=A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}
.
Then subbundles
D_{A}\subset E_{A}
reducing to
D_{B}\subset E_{B}
over
B

are in natural bijection with homomorphisms of bundles
D_{0}\to I\otimes_{\C}(E_{0}/D_{0})
over
X_{0}
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathfrak{m}}I=0
, we have a natural identification

I\cdot D_{A}\simeq I\otimes_{A}D_{A}\simeq I\otimes_{\C}(\C\otimes_{A}D_{A})\simeq I\otimes_{\C}D_{0}.

Thus,
D_{A}
is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}\otimes A
-submodule in
A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}
containing
I\otimes_{\C}D_{0}
and reducing to
D_{B}
modulo
I
.
Furthermore, since
D/I\cdot D_{A}=D_{B}
embeds into
E_{B}=(A/I)\otimes_{\C}E_{0}
, we have

D_{A}\cap I\otimes_{\C}E_{0}=I\cdot D_{A}=I\otimes_{\C}D_{0}.

Let
\pi:A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}\to B\otimes_{\C}E_{0}
be the projection. Let us consider the
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
-submodule

D^{\prime}_{A}:=\pi^{-1}(1\otimes D_{0})\cap D_{A}\subset A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}.

Since
D^{\prime}_{A}
contains
I\cdot D_{A}
and
\pi
induces a surjection from
A\cdot D^{\prime}_{A}
to
D_{A}/I\cdot D_{A}=A\otimes_{\C}D_{0}
, we see that

D_{A}=A\cdot D^{\prime}_{A},

so
D_{A}
is determined by
D^{\prime}_{A}
.

But
D^{\prime}_{A}
is in turn determined by the
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
-submodule

\overline{D}_{A}:=D^{\prime}_{A}/(I\otimes_{\C}D_{0})\subset A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}/(I\otimes_{\C}D_{0}),

which projects to
1\otimes D_{0}\subset(A/I)\otimes_{\C}E_{0}
.
We have a natural exact sequence of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
-modules

0\to I\otimes_{\C}(E_{0}/D_{0})\to A\otimes_{\C}E_{0}/(I\otimes_{\C}D_{0})\to(A/I)\otimes_{\C}E_{0}\to 0

equipped with a spitting
1\otimes v\mapsto 1\otimes v
over
1\otimes E_{0}
. Hence, we can view
\overline{D}_{A}
as an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
-submodule
of
I\otimes_{\C}(E_{0}/D_{0})\oplus 1\otimes D_{0}
, that intersects the first summand trivially. In other words,
\overline{D}_{A}
is the graph of a homomorphism

D_{0}\to I\otimes_{\C}(E_{0}/D_{0})
, which gives the claimed bijection.
∎

We consider the functor
\operatorname{Def}(X_{0})
from
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
to the category of sets associating with
A
the set of isomorphism classes of
deformations of
X_{0}
over
A
as an affine supercurve.
A deformation of a smooth supercurve
(X_{0},{\mathcal{D}}_{0})
over
A\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
consists of a superscheme
X_{A}
, flat over
A
, and reducing to
X_{0}
over
\C
,
as well as a distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{A}/\operatorname{Spec}(A)}
reducing to
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
. Note that
(X_{A},{\mathcal{D}}_{A})
will then automatically be a smooth supercurve over
A
(since
both smoothness and surjectivity of the map (
2.1
) are open conditions).

Below we will use the fact that a group valued functor on local Artinian
\C
-algebras, satisfying (H1) and (H2) is automatically smooth (see
[
18
, Thm. 7.19]
),
or rather a superanalog of this fact for group values functors on
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
X_{0}
be a smooth supercurve over
\C
. Consider the functor on
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
that associates with
A
the group
\operatorname{Aut}_{0}(X_{A}/A)
of automorphisms
of
X_{A}=X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
as a supercurve over
A
, reducing to the identity over
\C
. Then this functor satisfies (H1) and (H2), hence it is smooth.

Proof.

It is well known that for any separated scheme
Y_{0}
of finite type over
\C
the functor of automorphisms of
Y_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
over
A
, reducing to the identity over
\C
satisfies
(H1) and (H2) (see
[
18
, Ex. 7.2]
). This result also holds for superschemes.
Since the functor
A\mapsto\operatorname{Aut}_{0}(X_{A}/A)
is a subfunctor in such a functor for
X_{0}
, it suffices to check condition (H1) for the functor
\operatorname{Aut}_{0}
.
In the situation of (H1), set
B=A^{\prime}\times_{A}A^{\prime\prime}
, and
suppose we have compatible automorphisms
\alpha_{A^{\prime}}
and
\alpha_{A^{\prime\prime}}
of
X_{A^{\prime}}
and
X_{A^{\prime\prime}}
as supercurves. Then we know that they come from an automorphism

\alpha_{B}
of
X_{B}
as a superscheme over
B
. We claim that
\alpha_{B}
automatically preserves the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}:=B\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subset B\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}}
.
Indeed, since
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}={\mathcal{D}}_{A^{\prime}}\times_{{\mathcal{D}}_{A}}{\mathcal{D}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}
,
this follows immediately from the fact that
\alpha_{A^{\prime}}
and
\alpha_{A^{\prime\prime}}
preserve the distributions
{\mathcal{D}}_{A^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{A^{\prime\prime}}
, respectively.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

(i) Every deformation
X_{A}
of
X_{0}
over
A\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
is isomorphic to a trivial deformation

X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
, with the distribution induced by that on
X_{0}
.

(ii) Given a surjection
A\to B
in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
, and a deformation
X_{A}
of
X_{0}
over
A
, any superconformal automorphism
of
X_{B}=X_{A}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}A}\operatorname{Spec}B
lifts to a superconformal automorphism of
X_{A}
.

(iii) Analogs of (i) and (ii) hold for deformations of an affine supercurve
X_{0}
with an NS-puncture
P_{0}\subset X_{0}
.

(iv) For a surjection
A\to B
in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
any superconformal automorphism of
B(\!(z)\!)[\theta]
(with standard
\delta
) lifts to a superconformal
automorphism of
A(\!(z)\!)[\theta]
.

Proof.

(i) Let
X_{0}=\operatorname{Spec}(S_{0})
,
X_{A}=\operatorname{Spec}(S_{A})
.
Let also
{\mathfrak{m}}\subset A
be the
maximal ideal (which is nilpotent).
We have an exact sequence

0\to{\mathfrak{m}}S_{A}\to S_{A}\to S_{0}\to 0.

In particular,
S_{A}^{+}
is a nilpotent extension of
S_{0}^{+}
in the category of commutative
\C
-algebras.
Since
S_{0}^{+}
is a smooth finitely generated
\C
-algebra, by the infinitesimal lifting property, there exists a section

\sigma^{+}:S_{0}^{+}\to S_{A}^{+},

which is a homomorphism of
\C
-algebras.

We know that
S_{0}^{-}
is a locally free
S_{0}^{+}
-module of rank
1
. In particular,
S_{0}^{-}
is projective,
so we can choose an
S_{0}^{+}
-module splitting

\sigma^{-}:S_{0}^{-}\to S_{A}^{-}

of the projection
S_{A}^{-}\to S_{0}^{-}
, where we view
S_{A}^{-}
as an
S_{0}^{+}
-module via the embedding
\sigma^{+}
. Furthermore, we claim that
\sigma^{-}(S_{0}^{-})\cdot\sigma^{-}(S_{0}^{-})=0
in
S_{A}
.
Indeed, this follows from the fact that locally
S_{0}^{-}
is generated by one element.
Hence,
\sigma=(\sigma^{+},\sigma^{-}):S_{0}\to S_{A}
is a homomorphism of superalgebras.

From this we get a homomorphism
f:S_{0}\otimes_{\C}A\to S_{A}
of
A
-algebras which induces an isomorphism after tensoring with
A/{\mathfrak{m}}
.
Hence,
f
induces isomorphisms

S_{0}\otimes_{\C}{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{i+1}\to S_{A}\otimes_{A}{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{i+1}

for
i\geq 0
.
Since
S_{A}
is flat over
A
, we have isomorphisms
{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}S_{A}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{i+1}S_{A}\simeq S_{A}\otimes_{A}{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}/{\mathfrak{m}}^{i+1}
.
Since
{\mathfrak{m}}^{N}=0
for some
N>0
, the descending induction on
i
shows that
f
induces an isomorphism

S_{0}\otimes_{\C}{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}\to{\mathfrak{m}}^{i}S_{A}.

Hence,
f
is an isomorphism.

Now let
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}
be the distribution giving the supercurve
structure on
X_{0}
, and let us set
X_{A}=X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
.
It remains to show that any distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{A}/A}

giving a supercurve structure over
A
, deforming
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
,
is isomorphic to the pull-back of
A\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
under some automorphism of
X_{A}
trivial on
X_{0}
.

We can assume that for some ideal
I\subset A
, such that
{\mathfrak{m}}I=0
(so
A\to A/I
is a small extension),
the assertion holds for
A/I
,
so let

{\mathcal{D}}_{A}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{A}/A}\simeq A\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}

be a subbundle of rank
0|1
, which reduces to
A/I\otimes{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
over
A/I
.
By Lemma
3.13
,
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
corresponds to a homomorphism

f:{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\to I\otimes_{\C}({\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0}),

so that
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
is generated over
A
by sections of the form
1\otimes x+\widetilde{f(x)}
, for
x\in{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
, where
\widetilde{f(x)}\in I\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}

is a representative of
f(x)
.

On the other hand, any
I
-valued vector field
v\in I\otimes H^{0}(X_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k})
induces
an automorphism
\alpha_{v}
of
X_{A}
trivial on
X_{A/I}
: its action on functions is
\phi\mapsto\phi+v(\phi)
.
The automorphism
\alpha_{v}
acts on vector fields on
X_{A}
by
w\mapsto w+[v,w]
, so it changes the distribution
A\otimes_{\C}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
to the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}

generated over
A
by sections of the form
1\otimes x+[v,x]
. In other words,
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
corresponds to
the homomorphism
f_{v}:{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\to I\otimes_{\C}({\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0})
induced by the Lie bracket with
v
.
The condition that the map (
2.1
) is an isomorphism for
X_{0}
is equivalent to the condition that the map

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathcal{D}}_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0}):v\mapsto x\mapsto[v,x]

is an isomorphism. Hence, there is a unique global section
v
of
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\otimes I
, such that
f_{v}=f
, so the automorphism
\alpha_{v}
sends
A\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}}{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
to
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
,
as required.

(ii) By (i) all deformations are trivial. Now the assertion follows from Lemma
3.14
.

(iii) Let
\phi:S_{A}\to A
be the homomorphism corresponding to the NS-puncture, deforming

\phi_{0}:S_{0}\to k
. By (i), we can assume that
S_{A}=S_{0}\otimes_{\C}A
. We claim that there exists an automorphism of
S_{A}

over
A
, trivial on
S_{0}
, and compatible with the superconformal structure,
transforming
\phi_{0}\otimes A
to
\phi
.

It is enough to check this assuming that

\phi=\phi_{0}\otimes A\mod I
, where
{\mathfrak{m}}I=0
. Then
\phi-\phi_{0}\otimes A
is given by a
\phi_{0}
-derivation
S_{0}\to I
,
i.e., by an
I
-valued tangent vector at
P_{0}\in X_{0}
. It remains to extend this tangent vector to an
I
-valued
superconformal vector field (see Lemma
3.12
)
and consider the corresponding automorphism of
X_{A}
.

The fact about automorphisms follows similarly to (ii) from smoothness of the corresponding group scheme.

(iv) The proof is similar to (ii): the functor associating with
A
the group of superconformal automorphisms of
A(\!(z)\!)[\theta]
is a deformation
functor. Since we work over
\C
, it is smooth.
∎

3.6.
Neighborhood of Ramond puncture

Let
X_{0}
be an affine supercurve over
\C
with one Ramond puncture
R_{0}\subset X_{0}
, and let

{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}
be the structure distribution (so that (
2.2
) is an isomorphism).

Lemma 3.16
.

The natural morphism of sheaves

{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{D}}_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0}):v\mapsto(v_{0}\mapsto[v,v_{0}]\mod{\mathcal{D}}_{0})

is surjective with respect to étale topology. Hence, we have an exact sequence of sheaves in classical topology

0\to{\mathcal{T}}^{sc}_{X_{0}/k}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{D}}_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0})\to 0

and the induced map on global sections

H^{0}(X_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{O}}}({\mathcal{D}}_{0},{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0})

is surjective

Proof.

Locally in étale topology we can assume that
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
be generated by
v_{0}=\partial_{\theta}+\theta z\partial_{z}
(see Lemma
2.10
).
Then
\partial_{z}
projects to a basis of
{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}
.
Thus, it is enough to check that for every function
a=a(z,\theta)
there exists
v
with

[v,v_{0}]=a\partial_{z}\mod{\mathcal{D}}_{0}.

We can represent every
a
in the form
a=c_{0}+c_{1}\theta+bz
for some function
b
and some constants

c_{0},c_{1}
. It remains to note that

[(c_{0}\theta+c_{1})\partial_{z}+bv_{0},v_{0}]=(\pm c_{0}+c_{1}\theta+2bz)\partial_{z}.

The last statement is a consequence of the vanishing
H^{1}(X_{0},{\mathcal{T}}^{sc}_{X_{0}/k})=0
which itself follows from the fact that

{\mathcal{T}}^{sc}_{X_{0}}
is isomorphic to a coherent sheaf (see Proposition
3.9
).
∎

We have the following analog of Lemma
3.15
.

Lemma 3.17
.

(i) Every deformation
(X_{A},R_{A})
of
(X_{0},R_{0})
over
A\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
is isomorphic to the trivial deformation

(X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A),R_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A))
, with the distribution induced by that on
X_{0}
.

(ii) Given a surjection
A\to B
in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
, and a deformation
(X_{A},R_{A})
of
(X_{0},R_{0})
over
A
, any automorphism
of
(X_{B},X_{R})
(obtained by the base change to
B
) lifts to an automorphism of
(X_{A},R_{A})
.

Proof.

(i)
As we have seen in Lemma
3.15
(i),
we can assume that
X_{A}=X_{0}\times\operatorname{Spec}(A)
. We have a distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{A}/A}
deforming
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}
.
It is enough to check that
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
is obtained from
{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\otimes A

by an automorphism of
X_{A}
, trivial on
X_{0}
.
We can assume that for some ideal
I\subset A
, such that
{\mathfrak{m}}I=0
, the assertion holds for
A/I
,
so
{\mathcal{D}}_{A}
corresponds to a homomorphism

f_{\mathcal{D}}:{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{X_{0}/k}/{\mathcal{D}}_{0}\otimes I.

Now the assertion follows from Lemma
3.16
.

(ii) As before, this follows from (i) and from smoothness of the corresponding group scheme.
∎

3.7.
Unobstructedness

Recall that a morphism of deformation functors
\operatorname{Def}_{1}\to\operatorname{Def}_{2}
is called
smooth
if for every small extension
B\to A
in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
,
the natural map

\operatorname{Def}_{1}(B)\to\operatorname{Def}_{1}(A)\times_{\operatorname{Def}_{2}(A)}\operatorname{Def}_{2}(B)

is surjective.

Lemma 3.18
.

(i) Let
X_{0}
be an affine stable supercurve over
\C
with a single NS node
q
and let
\operatorname{Def}(\hat{{\mathcal{O}}})
denote the functor of deformations of
the completion of the local ring of
X_{0}
at
q
,

\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}\simeq\C[\![z_{1},z_{2}]\!][\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]/(z_{1}z_{2},\theta_{1}z_{2},\theta_{2}z_{1},\theta_{1}\theta_{2}).

Assume that there exists a function
f_{0}
on
X_{0}
such that in the formal neighborhood of
q
one has

f_{0}\equiv z_{1}+z_{2}\mod(z_{1},z_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})^{2}
, and the principal open affine
D(f_{0})\subset X_{0}
coincides with
X_{0}\setminus\{q\}
.
Then the natural morphism of deformation functors

\operatorname{Def}(X_{0})\to\operatorname{Def}(\hat{{\mathcal{O}}})

is smooth.

(ii) Similar assertions hold for am affine stable supercurve with a single Ramond node.

Proof.

(i) First, let us introduce the following notation. For
A\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
and
t\in{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
, we set

\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}:=A[\![z_{1},z_{2}]\!][\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]/(z_{1}z_{2}+t^{2},z_{1}\theta_{2}-t\theta_{1},z_{2}\theta_{1}+t\theta_{2},\theta_{1}\theta_{2}).

Step 1
. Let
A\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
,
t
be an element such that
t^{n}=0
. Let us consider a homomorphism of
A
-algebras,

\gamma:\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}\to A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]:\phi(z_{1},z_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto(\phi(z_{1},-t^{2}/z_{2},\theta_{1},t\theta_{1}/z_{1}),\phi(-t^{2}/z_{2},-t\theta_{2}/z_{2},z_{2},\theta_{2})).

Note that this is well-defined since
t^{n}=0
. It is also easy to see that
\gamma
is injective,
and the elements

(z_{1}^{-i},z_{1}^{-i}\theta_{1},z_{2}^{-i},z_{2}^{-i}\theta_{2})_{i\leq 0}

project to a basis of the quotient by the image of
\gamma
, as an
A
-module.
We claim that for any element
f\in\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}
such that
f\equiv z_{1}+z_{2}\mod(z_{1},z_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})^{2}
,

\gamma
is identified with the localization map

\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}\to\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}[f^{-1}].

Indeed, since the multiplication by
f
is invertible on
A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]
, it is enough to check that
for every
(p,q)\in A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]
one has
f^{N}(p,q)\in\operatorname{im}(\gamma)
for sufficiently large
N
.
But this follows from the inclusion

z_{1}^{n}A[\![z_{1}]\!][\theta_{1}]\oplus z_{2}^{n}A[\![z_{2}]\!][\theta_{2}]\subset\operatorname{im}(\gamma).

Note that this entire picture is obtained by the change of coefficients
\C[T]/(T^{n})\to A
from the standard picture described in Sec.
3.2
.
It follows that we have a structure derivation

\delta:\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}\to\omega_{\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{A,t}/A},

such that after inverting
z_{1}
or
z_{2}
it is given by the formula (
3.2
).

Step 2
. Let
X_{B}
be a deformation of
X_{0}
over
B\in\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
. Then for any function
f\in{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})
, lifting
f_{0}
,
the principal open affine
D(f)\subset X_{B}
is the trivial deformation of
D(f_{0})=X_{0}\setminus\{q\}
over
B
.
Furthermore, there exist an isomorphism of supercurves
D(f)\simeq\operatorname{Spec}(B)\times D(f_{0})
, and a superconformal isomorphism

\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X_{B},q}\simeq\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{B,t},

(3.6)

for some element
t
of the maximal ideal in
B
, so that we have a cartesian diagram

{diagram}

Here
r_{B}
comes from a certain isomorphism
B\otimes\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))}\simeq B(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus B(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]
,
where we equip
{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))
with the topology using powers of
I_{q}
, the ideal of a node in
{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})
, and consider the completion.

Indeed, it is clear that
D(f)
is a deformation of
D(f_{0})
as a supercurve.
Since
D(f_{0})
is a smooth affine supercurve, by Lemma
3.15
(i) all its deformations are trivial.
The existence of a superconformal isomorphism (
3.6
) follows from Theorem
3.3
.
Finally, by Step 1, we have

B(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus B(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]\simeq\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{B,t}[f^{-1}]\simeq\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{B,t}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})}{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})[f^{-1}]\simeq\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{B,t}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})}(B\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))),

which is isomorphic to the completion of
B\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))
with respect to the
I_{q}
-adic topology.

Step 3
. Now let
A\to B
be a small extension in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
.
We claim that there exists a homomorphism

r_{A}:A\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))\to A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]

lifting
r_{B}
and compatible with derivations. Now for an element

\widetilde{t}\in A
lifting
t\in B
,
let us define the ring
\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}
as the fibered product

\begin{diagram}

(3.7)

Then we claim that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}={\mathcal{O}}(X_{A})
for some deformation
X_{A}
of
X_{0}
inducing
X_{B}
(so there is also a derivation
\delta
on
X_{A}
).

First, we have a superconformal isomorphism

\alpha_{0}:\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))}\simeq\C(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus\C(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}].

Hence, the map
r_{B}
corresponds to the composition of superconformal isomorphisms

B\otimes\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))}\rTo{B\otimes\alpha_{0}}B(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus B(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]\rTo{\alpha_{B}}B(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus B(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]

for some
\alpha_{B}
. Now we define
r_{A}
using the composition of superconformal isomorphisms

A\otimes\widehat{{\mathcal{O}}(D(f_{0}))}\rTo{A\otimes\alpha_{0}}A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}]\rTo{\alpha_{A}}A(\!(z_{1})\!)[\theta_{1}]\oplus A(\!(z_{2})\!)[\theta_{2}],

where
\alpha_{A}
is some lift of
\alpha_{B}
. We know that such a lift exists by Lemma
3.15
(iv).

Since the lower horizontal arrow in (
3.7
) is a split embedding of
A
-modules, so is the upper horizontal arrow.
Hence,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}
is a projective
A
-module. Applying the reduction
?\otimes_{A}B
to our diagram we recover the
cartesian diagram from Step 2, so
\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}\otimes_{A}B\simeq{\mathcal{O}}(X_{B})
.

Finally, we observe that we have a cartesian diagram of the dualizing sheaves parallel to diagram (
3.7
).
Since both
r_{A}
and
\gamma
are compatible with the derivations, we get a
a well defined derivation
\delta:\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}\to\omega_{\widetilde{{\mathcal{O}}}/A}
.

(ii) The proof is similar to (i).
∎

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})
be a stable supercurve with punctures over
\C
. Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{m}
be all the nodes of
X
.
Then the morphism of deformation functors

\operatorname{Def}(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})\to\prod_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{Def}({\mathcal{O}}_{X,q_{i}})

(3.8)

is smooth.
The induced morphism of tangent spaces fits into an exact sequence

0\to H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}})\to T_{\operatorname{Def}(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}T_{\operatorname{Def}(\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,q_{i}})}\to 0.

(3.9)

Proof.

Roughly speaking, the idea is that away from the nodes, locally all deformations are trivial, so deformations are classified by appropriate noncommutative
H^{1}

sets. Now we use the vanishing of the relevant
H^{2}
.

Suppose
B\to B/I=A
is a small extension in
\operatorname{Art}_{\C}
,
and we are given a deformation
X_{A}
of
X
over
A
(as a punctured supercurve), and
liftings of the induced deformations of
\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,q_{i}}
to deformations of singularities over
B
. To prove smoothness of the morphism (
3.8
),
we need to construct a deformation
X_{B}
over
B
lifting
X_{A}
and inducing the given deformations of the singularities over
B
.

Let us cover
X_{A}
by affine opens
U_{iA}
such that for
i=1,\ldots,m
,
U_{i}:=U_{iA}\times_{\operatorname{Spec}(A)}\operatorname{Spec}(\C)
contains
q_{i}
and does not contain other
nodes or punctures, while for
i>m
,
U_{i}
’s lies in the smooth locus and
contains at most one R-puncture or NS-puncture.

By Lemma
3.18
, for
i=1,\ldots,m
, we can extend the deformation
U_{iA}
over
A
to a deformation

U_{iB}
over
B
, inducing the given deformation of the singularity at
q_{i}
.
By the results of Sec.
3.5
and
3.6
all the local deformations

U_{iA}
, for
i>m
, are trivial, so we can
lift them to trivial deformations
U_{iB}
over
B
. Furthermore, all the intersections
U_{ij}
are smooth and contain
no punctures, so all their deformations are trivial, and we have some isomorphisms
\alpha_{ijB}
between
the deformations of
U_{ij}
over
B
, induced from
U_{iB}
and
U_{jB}
.
The induced isomorphisms
\alpha_{ijA}
may differ from the ones coming from
X_{A}
, but we can correct
each
\alpha_{ijB}
using Lemma
3.15
(ii) to make sure that
\alpha_{ijA}
are the ones coming from
X_{A}
.

Thus, considering
\alpha_{kiB}\alpha_{jkB}\alpha_{ijB}
over triple intersections we get a
2
-cocycle with values in

I\otimes{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}}
. Since the corresponding
H^{2}
vanishes, this
2
-cocycle is a coboundary.
Hence, we can correct
\alpha_{ijB}
, so that they become compatible on triple intersections (without changing
\alpha_{ijA}
).
Thus, our data will give a deformation
X_{B}
of
X
(as a punctured supercurve).

By smoothness, we have surjectivity in sequence (
3.9
). It remains to observe that
H^{1}(X,{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}})

is identified with the space of locally trivial infinitesimal deformations, i.e., those infinitesimal deformations of
(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})
that induce trivial deformations
of the singularities.
∎

Using the fact that the deformations of nodal singularities of supercurves are smooth (see Corollary
3.4
),
we derive the following

Corollary 3.20
.

The functor
\operatorname{Def}(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})
is smooth.

4.
Square of the relative canonical bundle

In this section we will define the line bundle
\omega^{2}_{X/S}
on certain families of stable supercurves, as an extension from the smooth locus.
We show that it is useful in finding an ample line bundle over
X
, as well as due to its relation to the sheaf on infinitesimal automorphisms.

4.1.
Local freeness and ampleness

It is well known that for a stable curve with punctures
(C,p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})
over
S_{0}
, the line bundle

\omega_{C/S_{0}}^{log}=\omega_{C/S_{0}}(p_{1}+\ldots+p_{n})
is relatively ample.
Our goal is to find an analogous construction for families of stable supercurves.
The problem is that for a stable supercurve
X/S
the relative dualizing sheaf
\omega_{X/S}
is not necessarily locally free.

We show that this problem can be solved by extending
\omega^{2}_{X/S}
from the smooth locus.

Note if
X/S
is a prestable supercurve and
q\in X_{s}
is a node of some fiber then the completion
\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{X,q}
gives a formal deformation of the node
singularity in
X_{s}
with the base
\hat{{\mathcal{O}}}_{S,s}
, hence, it induces a map from the formal neighborhood of
s
in
S
to the base of the miniversal deformation of the node
(described in Theorem
3.3
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
f:X\to S
be a family of prestable supercurves inducing a smooth morphism to the base of the miniversal deformation of every node
in a fiber. Let
j:U\hookrightarrow X
be the open complement to the locus of nodes in fibers. Then for any
n\in\Z
, the sheaf

\omega_{X/S}^{2n}:=j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2n}
is a line bundle on
X
. For a morphism
u:S^{\prime}\to S
such that the induced family
X^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}

also satisfies the above assumption, there is a natural isomorphism

v^{*}\omega_{X/S}^{2n}\rTo{\sim}\omega_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{2n},

(4.1)

where
v:X^{\prime}\to X
is the induced morphism. In the case when
S
is even, and
(C,L)
is the underlying family of curves with spin structures, we have

\omega_{X/S}^{2n}|_{C}\simeq\omega_{C/S}^{n}\oplus\omega_{C/S}^{n}\otimes L=p^{*}\omega_{C/S}^{n},

where
p:X\to C
is the natural projection.
In particular,

\omega_{X/S}^{2n}|_{C}\simeq\omega_{C/S}^{n}.

Proof.

We claim that under our assumptions
X
is Cohen-Macaulay near the nodes. Indeed, by assumption,
S
is smooth near
every point
s
with singular fiber
X_{s}
. Since the morphism
f
is Cohen-Macaulay, the claim follows from

[
30
, Lem. 37.21.4]
.
Since the complement to
U
has codimension
\geq 2
in
X
, by Lemma
2.5
(i),
we see that the natural map

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to j_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}

is an isomorphism. We will use this fact below.

Let us first prove that
j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2}
is a line bundle
for the miniversal deformations of the nodes, i.e., when
S=\operatorname{Spec}k[t]
and
X
is given as in
(
3.2.1
) and (
3.2.2
).

For a Ramond node,
\omega_{X/S}
is a line bundle, hence,
\omega_{X/S}^{\otimes 2}
is also a line bundle, and

\omega_{X/S}^{\otimes 2}\simeq j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2}.

For an NS node, the total space
C
of the underlying usual family of curves is the quadratic cone

xy=-t^{2}
, and
U^{{\operatorname{red}}}
is the complement to the singular point
q
in
C
. Furthermore, we have

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{C}\oplus L,\ \ \omega_{X/S}\simeq L\oplus\omega_{C/S},

where
L
is a CM-sheaf of rank
1
over
C
(see Lemma
2.6
). Over
U
we have

\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2}\simeq\omega_{U^{{\operatorname{red}}}/S}\oplus\omega_{U^{{\operatorname{red}}}/S}\otimes L|_{U}.

We can compute the push-forward
j_{*}
componentwise. Since the complement of
U
has codimension
\geq 2
,

\omega_{C/S}
is locally free and
L
is a CM-sheaf with full support, by Lemma
2.5
, we get

j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2}\simeq\omega_{C/S}\oplus\omega_{C/S}\otimes L,

which is just the pull-back of
\omega_{C/S}
under the natural projection
X\to C
.
Hence,
j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{\otimes 2}
is locally free of rank
1
.

To prove the result in the general case, i.e., for an arbitary family
X/S
inducing a smooth morphism to the base of the miniversal deformation of every node,
we observe that the question is local, and by Lemma
3.5
, an étale neighborhood
B
of
X
will have a smooth morphism
t:B\to X_{0}

to the miniversal deformation of the node
X_{0}/S_{0}
, so that
B\cap U=t^{-1}(U_{0})
, where
U_{0}\subset X_{0}
is the complement to the node. Then the base change morphism

t^{*}j_{0*}\omega_{U_{0}/S_{0}}^{2}\to j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{2}

is an isomorphism, hence,
j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{2}
is locally free.
The isomorphism (
4.1
) follows from this and from the compatilibity of the base change morphisms for the maps to
X_{0}

from étale neighborhoods of nodes in
X^{\prime}
and in
X
.
∎

Now we recall that for every NS-puncture
P_{i}
,
there is a canonical divisor
D_{i}
with the same support (see Sec.
2.5
). So, we can define the line bundle on
X
,

{\mathcal{L}}_{X/S}:=\omega_{X/S}^{\otimes 2}(\sum D_{i}+\sum R_{j}).

Let
f:X\to S
be a proper morphism of Noetherian superschemes.

Definition 4.2
.

We say that a line bundle (of rank
1|0
)
L
over
X
is
strongly relatively ample over

S

if there exists
n>0
,
a supervector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
over
S
and a closed embedding
\phi:X\to{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}})
over
S
,
such that
L^{n}\simeq\phi^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(1)
.

For a superscheme
S
we denote by
S_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
the usual scheme with the same underlying topological space as
S
and with the sheaf of rings

{\mathcal{O}}_{S}/{\mathcal{N}}_{S}
, where
{\mathcal{N}}_{S}
is the ideal generated by odd functions.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
f:X\to S
be a family of stable supercurves inducing a smooth morphism to the base of the miniversal deformation of every node
in a fiber. Then the line bundle
{\mathcal{L}}_{X/S}
is strongly relatively ample.

Proof.

Let
C\to S_{0}
be the underlying usual family of stable curves over
S_{0}=S_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
.
Since
f
is flat, by Proposition
A.2
of the Appendix, it suffices to prove that
L:={\mathcal{L}}_{X/S}|_{C}
is strongly relatively ample over
S_{0}
.
Since
L
is a line bundle, the natural map

L_{X/S}\to j_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}j_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{*}L_{X/S}

is an isomorphism, where
j_{{\operatorname{bos}}}:U_{{\operatorname{bos}}}\to C
is the embedding of the complement to the nodes.
We have

j_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{*}L_{X/S}\simeq\omega_{U_{{\operatorname{bos}}}/S_{0}}(\sum D_{i{\operatorname{bos}}}+\sum R_{j{\operatorname{bos}}}).

Hence,

L_{X/S}\simeq\omega_{C/S_{0}}(\sum D_{i{\operatorname{bos}}}+\sum R_{j{\operatorname{bos}}}),

which is relatively ample by a classical result on stable curves (see
[
14
, Thm. 1.2]
,
[
3
, Lem. 6.1]
).
∎

4.2.
The sheaf of infinitesimal symmetries

Now we can extend Proposition
3.9
to the case of stable supercurves.

Theorem 4.4
.

Let
(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})\to S
be a family stable supercurve with punctures, where
(P_{i})_{i\in I}
are NS punctures
and
(R_{j})_{j\in J}
are Ramond punctures, inducing a smooth morphism to the base of the miniversal deformation of every node in a fiber.
Then one has a natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})/S}\simeq\omega_{X/S}^{-2}(-\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}-2\sum_{j\in J}R_{j}),

where
\omega_{X/S}^{-2}
is the line bundle defined in Theorem
4.1

and
D_{i}\subset X
is the divisor associated with the NS puncture
P_{i}
(see Sec.
2.5
).

Proof.

Over the smooth locus this holds by Proposition
3.9
.
Now let us show that the natural map

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\to j_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{U/S},

where
j:U\to X
is the embedding of the smooth locus, is an isomorphism.
Since the condition on
v\in{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}
to belong to

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}
can be imposed only over the smooth locus,
it is enough to check that the natural map

{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}\to j_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{U/S}

is an isomorphism. But this follows immediately from the fact that
j_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{U/S}
can be identified with relative
derivations of
j_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}
and the isomorphism
j_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{U}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

Note that neither
{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}
nor
{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}
are compatible with the base change. So in the situation of Theorem
4.4
the restriction of

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}
to a fiber
X_{s}
, which is a stable supercurve with at least one node, is a line bundle on
X_{s}
, whereas
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s}}
is not
(since
T_{C}
is not locally free for a nodal curve
C
).

5.
Proof of Theorem A

To prove Theorem
A
we need to check that
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{g,n_{NS},n_{R}}
is a stack with representable diagonal, with an étale atlas,
and that it is smooth and proper over
\C
.

5.1.
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{g,n_{NS},n_{R}}
is a limit preserving stack

A family of stable supercurves is given by a superscheme
X\to S
, smooth of dimension
1|1
,
together with a morphism
\delta:\Omega_{X/S}\to\omega_{X/S}
and sections
p_{i}:S\to X
(the R-punctures can
be recovered from
\delta
). The fact that isomorphisms between two families over
S
form a sheaf in étale topology
is proved in the standard way (and also follows from the representability proved below).
The étale descent for such families follows as in the classical case from the existence of the relatively ample bundle which we proved
in Theorem
4.3
(see
[
17
, Sec. 4.3.3]
; see also
[
27
, Sec. 7.2.1]
).

The fact that our stack
{\mathcal{M}}
is limit preserving, i.e., if
S=\operatorname{Spec}(A)

with
A=\varinjlim_{i}A_{i}
, then
{\mathcal{M}}(A)\simeq\varinjlim_{i}{\mathcal{M}}(A_{i})
, is proved essentially by the same arguments as in the case of the
moduli stack of usual curves, see
[
27
, Lem. 7.23]
for details.

5.2.
Representability of the diagonal

We need to check that for a pair of families of stable supercurves

X\to S
and
Y\to T
, there is an algebraic space locally of finite type over
\C
, classifying isomorphisms
between
X_{s}
and
Y_{t}
as abstract superschemes. Passing to the induced families over
S\times T
, we can assume that
we have two families
X\to S
and
Y\to S
over the same base, and we need to check
that the functor
\operatorname{Isom}(X,Y)
is representable by an algebraic space over
S
. In fact, we will check that it is representable by a superscheme over
S
.

For this we can use relative projectivity of the morphisms
X\to S
and
Y\to S
which holds by Theorem
4.3

and the standard approach via the Hilbert (super)schemes (the construction of Hilbert superschemes for
projective morphisms of superschemes is discussed in
[
7
, Sec. 4]
and in
[
27
, Sec. 7]
).
Namely, as in the classical case, the idea is that to an isomorphism
X_{s}\to Y_{s}

we can associate its graph, which is a closed subscheme in
X_{s}\times Y_{s}
.
In more detail, first, let
{\mathcal{H}}
be the relative Hilbert superscheme over
S
parametrizing subschemes in
X_{s}\times Y_{s}

(with the same Hilbert series as the Hilbert series of
X_{s}
with respect to a large power of
{\mathcal{L}}_{X/S}|_{X_{s}}
).
Let

Z\subset X\times_{S}\times Y\times_{S}{\mathcal{H}}

be the universal subscheme.
Let us consider the projections

p_{X}:Z\to X\times_{S}{\mathcal{H}},\ \ p_{Y}:Z\to Y\times_{S}{\mathcal{H}}.

Then there is a universal open subscheme
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{H}}

over which
p_{X}
and
p_{Y}
become isomorphisms (this is proved as in
[
17
, Thm. 5.22]
).
Then it is easy to see that
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
represents
\operatorname{Isom}_{\operatorname{sSch}/S}(X,Y)
.

Next, assume that our stable supercurves
X
and
Y
over the same base are equipped with the matching number of punctures of each type,

(P_{i}^{X},R_{j}^{X})
and
(P_{i}^{Y},R_{j}^{Y})
.
Let
Z\subset X\times_{S}\times Y\times_{S}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
be the graph of the universal isomorphism.
Then we have the induced sections
Z\times_{X}P_{i}^{X}
and
Z\times_{Y}P_{i}^{Y}
of the family
Z\to{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
,
and induced divisors
Z\times_{X}R_{j}^{X}
and
Z\times_{Y}R_{j}^{Y}
in
Z
, which are smooth of dimension
0|1
over
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
. Let us set
P_{X}
(resp.,
P_{Y}
) be the subscheme of
Z
obtained as the disjoint union of these subschemes pulled back from
X
(resp.,
Y
). Then there exists the largest closed subscheme

{\mathcal{H}}_{2}\subset{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
such that
P_{X}
and
P_{Y}
coincide over
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
.
This is a consequence of the following easy Lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

(a) Let
f:F_{1}\to F_{2}
be a morphism of coherent sheaves over a superscheme
Z
, and let
Z\to S
be a morphism.
Assume that
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are flat over
S
. Then there exists the largest closed subscheme

T\subset S
such that
f=0
over
T
.

(b) Let
R
and
R^{\prime}
be subschemes in a superscheme
Z
, and let
Z\to S
be a flat morphism such that both
R
and
R^{\prime}
are flat over
S
.
Then there exists the largest closed subscheme

T\subset S
such that
R=R^{\prime}
over
T
.

Proof.

(a) Let
Z_{f}\subset Z
be the closed subscheme corresponding to the ideal
\ker(f:{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(F_{1},F_{2}))
. Then
T
is the schematic image of
Z_{f}
.

(b) We apply (a) to the morphisms
I_{R}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}/I_{R^{\prime}}
and
I_{R^{\prime}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}/I_{R}
and observe that these morphisms vanish if and only if
R=R^{\prime}
.
∎

Finally, we can define a closed subscheme
{\mathcal{H}}_{sc}\subset{\mathcal{H}}
that corresponds to superconformal isomorphisms.
To this end we observe that we can pull back to
Z
both morphisms
\delta_{X}:\Omega_{X/S}\to\omega_{X/S}(R_{X})
and
\delta_{Y}:\Omega_{Y/S}\to\omega_{Y/S}(R_{Y})
,
and then apply Lemma
5.1
to the difference.
Then
{\mathcal{H}}_{sc}
represents the sheaf of isomorphisms between families
X
and
Y
as stable supercurves with punctures.

5.3.
Construction of an étale atlas

The main point is to use the existence of the (purely even) Deligne-Mumford stack
\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}=\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}_{g,n_{NS},n_{R}}
parametrizing
stable curves with spin-structures (and punctures), constructed in
[
1
]
.

For every stable supercurve
X_{0}
over
\C
, we can find a family
\pi:{\mathcal{X}}_{0}\to B_{0}
of stable supercurves (with punctures) over an affine even base
B_{0}
,
and a
\C
-point
b\in B_{0}
such that we get
X_{0}
as a fiber of
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
over
b
,
such that the corresponding map
B_{0}\to\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}
is étale.

Let us consider the corresponding bundle over
B_{0}
,

{\mathcal{E}}:=R^{1}\pi_{*}({\mathcal{A}}^{-})=R^{1}\pi_{*}(\omega_{{\mathcal{C}}/B_{0}}^{-1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{i})),

where
{\mathcal{C}}\subset{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
is the corresponding usual curve over
B_{0}
with the relative spin-structure
{\mathcal{L}}

(recall that
{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
denotes the sheaf of odd infinitesimal automorphisms of
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}/B_{0}
). Note that the fact that
{\mathcal{E}}
is
locally free follows from the vanishing of
\pi_{*}({\mathcal{A}}^{-})
, i.e., from the absence of odd infinitesimal automorphisms of
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}/B_{0}
.

We define the superbase
B
as

B=\operatorname{Spec}_{B_{0}}({\bigwedge}^{\bullet}\Pi{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}).

In other words, we view
{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}
as extra odd coordinates.
Our goal is to extend
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
to a family
{\mathcal{X}}\to B
(possibly after changing
B_{0}
to an étale neighborhood of
b
).

Let
q_{1},\ldots,q_{N}
be a finite number of points in
C_{0}
, including all nodes,
q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}
,
such that there is an ample effective divisor
D
supported on
\{q_{1},\ldots,q_{N}\}
, and
q_{i}
are distinct from all the punctures.
Let also
q^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,q^{\prime}_{m}
be a set of smooth points in
C_{0}
, distinct from all the punctures and from
q_{i}
’s, such that

D^{\prime}=q^{\prime}_{1}+\ldots+q^{\prime}_{m}
is ample.

We claim that replacing
B_{0}
by an étale neighborhood of
b
, we can assume
the existence of relatively ample effective Cartier divisors
{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
, extending
D
and
D^{\prime}
,
such that
{\mathcal{D}}\cap{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}=\emptyset
.

Therefore, we have an affine cover of
{\mathcal{X}}_{0}
by two open sets,
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
defined as the complement to
D^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
defined as the complement to
D
.
Now, let us consider the tautological cohomology class

[c]\in H^{0}(B_{0},{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}\otimes R^{1}\pi_{*}({\mathcal{A}}^{-}))\simeq H^{1}({\mathcal{X}}_{0},\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{-}).

We can represent it by a Cech cocycle

c\in H^{0}({\mathcal{U}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{1},\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{-}).

Now we observe that there is a natural morphism of sheaves

\omega_{{\mathcal{C}}/B_{0}}^{-1}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}(-\sum p_{i}-\sum r_{i})={\mathcal{A}}^{-}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{0}/B_{0},P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet}}^{-}.

Hence, we can view odd sections of
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}\otimes{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
as an even derivation

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{0}}\to\Pi\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}

(relative to
B_{0}
). Thus,
c
gives rise to such a derivation over
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
.
Hence, we have a homomorphism

\exp(c):{\mathcal{O}}\to{\bigwedge}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\Pi\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee},

over
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
, reducing to identity modulo
{\bigwedge}^{\geq 1}
.
We can extend
\exp(c)
to the automorphism
\widetilde{c}
of

{\bigwedge}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}_{0}}}\Pi\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}
, identical on
\pi^{-1}{\bigwedge}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{B_{0}}}\Pi{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}
.
Now we define
{\mathcal{X}}
by gluing

{\mathcal{U}}_{i}\times_{B_{0}}B\simeq\operatorname{Spec}({\bigwedge}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{O}}}\Pi\pi^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\vee}|_{{\mathcal{U}}_{i}}),\ \ i=0,1,

using
\widetilde{c}
as an automorphism of
({\mathcal{U}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{1})\times_{B_{0}}B
.

Note that since on a smooth locus
c
acts by a derivation preserving the structure distribution
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}
,
the same is true for
\widetilde{c}
. Hence,
{\mathcal{X}}
has a natural structure of a stable supercurve over
B
(see Lemma
2.7
).

We claim that the corresponding map from
B
to the moduli stack
{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{M}}
of supercurves is étale near
b
.
Indeed, it is enough to check that the induced map on tangent spaces at
b
is an isomorphism.
The tangent space
T_{b}B
to
B
at
b
is given by

(T_{b}B)^{+}=T_{b}B_{0},\ \ (T_{b}B)^{-}={\mathcal{E}}_{b}=H^{1}(X_{0},{\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{X_{0}}).

The fact that the map on even tangent spaces is an isomorphism follows from the assumption that
B_{0}\to{\mathcal{S}}
is étale.
The map on odd tangent spaces corresponds to the natural map

H^{1}(X_{0},{\mathcal{A}}^{-}_{X_{0}})\to(T_{b}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{M}})^{-}.

The fact that it is an isomorphism follows from the exact sequence (
3.9
) since the last term of this sequence is purely even.

5.4.
Properties of the stack of stable supercurves

Corollary
3.20
shows that the stack of stable supercurves is smooth. Since it is of finite type (for each fixed genus and fixed number of punctures),
the fact that it is proper can be checked for its even part, i.e., for the stack of stable curves with spin structures. But this is known due to works of Cornalba
[
11
]

and Jarvis
[
22
]
.

6.
Kodaira-Spencer map

In this section we will study the behavior of the Kodaira-Spencer map in degenerating families of supercurves where the limiting curve acquires one NS or Ramond
node. This will later help us to calculate the canonical line bundle of the moduli stack of stable supercurves.
We begin with the classical case of a degenerating family of usual curve and then consider separately the cases of NS and Ramond nodes.

6.1.
Classical case

Let
\pi:C\to S
be a family of stable curves over a smooth affine base
S
, equipped with a smooth morphism
t:S\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.
We denote by
S_{0}\subset S
the divisor
t=0
and by
\pi_{0}:C_{0}\to S_{0}
the induced family over
S_{0}
.
We assume that there is a section
q:S_{0}\to C_{0}
such that the map
C\to S
is smooth away from
q(S_{0})
, and that the structure sheaf of the
completion of
C
along
q(S_{0})

is isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[\![x,y]\!]/(xy-t)
(so that the section
q
corresponds to
x=y=t=0
).
Below we will write simply
q
to denote the relative node
q(S_{0})\subset C_{0}
.

We would like to discuss the Kodaira-Spencer map for such a family.

Let us denote by
{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
the sheaf of derivations of
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
preserving the ideal generated by
t
.
Let also
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
denote the sheaf of derivations of
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
preserving the divisor
C_{0}
, i.e.,
preserving the ideal generated by
t
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
.
Finally, we denote by
{\mathcal{T}}_{C/S}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
the relative tangent sheaf.

Note that
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
can be identified with derivations
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
(since
\Omega_{S}
is locally free).
The subsheaf
\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
corresponds to derivations
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
sending
t
to
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}\cdot t
.
Thus, we have a natural morphism
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
sending a derivation of
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
to its restriction to
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
.
Similarly, we have a map
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}\to\pi_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}
sending a derivation of
{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}
to its restriction to
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S_{0}}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

(i) There are exact sequences

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C/S}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to 0

(6.1)

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}\to\pi_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}\to 0

(6.2)

(ii) For the closed point
x_{0}\subset S_{0}
, the natural map

{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}

(6.3)

is an isomorphism.

(iii) Let
j:C\setminus\{q\}\to C
denote the open embedding. Then the natural map
\omega_{C/S}\to j_{*}\omega_{C\setminus\{q\}/S}
is an isomorphism.
The corresponding natural map

\Omega_{C/S}\to j_{*}\Omega_{C\setminus\{q\}/S}\simeq j_{*}\omega_{C\setminus\{q\}/S}\simeq\omega_{C/S}

is injective with the cokernel isomorphic to
{\mathcal{O}}_{q}
.
Hence, the dual map

\omega_{C/S}^{-1}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C/S}

is an isomorphism.
Also, the coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
on
C
is locally free, and the sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}
on
C_{0}
is flat over
S
.

(iv) Let us consider the map

{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}/(t)=i_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}},

where
i:C_{0}\to C
is the natural embedding,
sending
v
to
v(t)\mod(t)
.
Then its image is
i_{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{q}
, where
{\mathcal{I}}_{q}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}
is the ideal sheaf of
q
, so we have
an exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{I}}_{q}\to 0

(6.4)

(v) There is an exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C}/{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\rTo{t}{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}/t{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}

Proof.

(i) For the first sequence, we have to check that the map
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
is surjective. This is clear away from
q
.
It remains to check this in the formal neighborhood of
q
. But we can extend the derivation
t\partial_{t}
to the derivation of
k[\![x,y,t]\!]/(xy-t)
induced by

x\partial_{x}+t\partial_{t}
.

It follows that the composition

{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to i_{*}\pi_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}

is surjective, which implies surjectivity of the map
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}\to\pi_{0}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}
, and hence, exactness of the second sequence.

(ii) This follows easily by a local computation near the node. Namely, locally a derivation
v
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}
is described
by a pair of functions
v(x)
and
v(y)
, satisfying
v(x)y+xv(y)=0
, or equivalently
v(x)\in(x)
,
v(y)\in(y)
, so
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}

is locally isomorphic to

{\mathcal{I}}_{q}\simeq x\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}\oplus y\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}.

Similarly
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}
is isomorphic to

{\mathcal{I}}_{q_{0}}\simeq x\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}}\oplus y\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}},

where
q_{0}
is the node in
C_{x_{0}}
.
The natural map (
6.3
) corresponds under these isomorphisms to the natural map

(x\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}\oplus y\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}})|_{{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}}}\to x\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}}\oplus y\cdot{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}},

which is an isomorphism.

(iii) The first assertion follows from the fact that
\omega_{C/S}
is locally free.
The injectivity of
\Omega_{C/S}\to\omega_{C/S}
only has to be checked in a formal neighborhood of
q
.
Then we can identify
\Omega_{C/S}
with
({\mathcal{O}}dx\oplus{\mathcal{O}}dy)/(xdy+ydx)
.
It is easy to see that any element of
\Omega_{C/S}
can be written uniquely as

f(t,x,y)dx+g(y)dy.

The map
\Omega_{C/S}\to\omega_{C/S}
is given by
dx\mapsto x\cdot{\mathbf{b}}
,
dy\mapsto-y\cdot{\mathbf{b}}
, where
{\mathbf{b}}
is a generator of
\omega_{C/S}
.
Hence, it sends the above element to

([f(t,x,y)x-g(y)y)\cdot{\mathbf{b}},

which is zero only if
f=g=0
.
The last assertion follows by dualizing the sequence

0\to\Omega_{C/S}\to\omega_{C/S}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{q}\to 0

and using the vanishing
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathcal{O}}_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{C})=\underline{\operatorname{Ext}}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{q},{\mathcal{O}}_{C})=0
.

The fact that
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
is locally free follows from the exact sequence (
6.1
).
The sheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}
is locally free away from the node. Thus, to check its flatness it is enough to consider it in the
formal neighborhood of the node. Then it can be identified with the subsheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}

consisting of
(f,g)
such that
f\in(x)
and
g\in(y)
, so it is locally free as
{\mathcal{O}}_{S_{0}}
-module.

(iv) Since the map
\pi:C\to S
is smooth away from
q
, it is enough to check the assertion in the formal neighborhood of the node.
Then we can extend
x,y
to formal coordinates
(x,y,t_{1},\ldots,t_{d})
on
C
. Since
t=xy
, the map in question
{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{C}/(t)
is given by

f\partial_{x}+g\partial_{y}+\sum f_{i}\partial_{t_{i}}\mapsto fy+gx\mod(t),

so its image is
{\mathcal{I}}_{q}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{0}}
.

(v) This follows easily from the fact that the kernel of the projection
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}
is
t{\mathcal{T}}_{C}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
.
∎

It follows that we can rewrite the exact sequence (
6.1
) as

0\to\omega^{-1}_{C/S}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to 0.

(6.5)

Hence, applying the functor
R\pi_{*}(\cdot)
and using the isomorphism
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{C}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
, we get the induced coboundary map

KS:{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}

(6.6)

which we call the
Kodaira-Spencer map
for our family, since it restricts to the usual Kodaira-Spencer map over
S\setminus S_{0}
.
Note that we also have a similar map coming from the sequence (
6.2
),

KS_{0}:{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}\to R^{1}\pi_{0*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}.

(6.7)

Let
x_{0}\in S_{0}
denote the origin, and let
C_{x_{0}}
be the corresponding curve with one node.
Then by Lemma
6.1
(ii), we have
{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}/S_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}
,
so the exact sequence (
6.2
) restricts to the standard exact sequence associated with the
embedding
C_{x_{0}}\hookrightarrow C_{0}
(with the trivial normal bundle):

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}}\to T_{x_{0}}S_{0}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}}\to 0.

We can look at the corresponding coboundary map

\kappa_{x_{0}}:T_{x_{0}}S_{0}\to H^{1}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}).

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume that the map
\kappa_{x_{0}}
is injective.
Then
R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}
is a vector bundle on
S

and the map (
6.6
) is an embedding of a subbundle.

Proof.

Since
C_{x_{0}}
is a stable curve, we have
H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},\omega^{-1})=0
, which implies by semicontinuity
that
\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}=0
, and by the base change theorem that

R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}
is locally free.
Thus, we have a long exact sequence

0\to\pi_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\rTo{KS}R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{C}\otimes{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to 0.

Note that
R^{1}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
is locally free of rank
g
. Also
{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
is a vector bundle on
C
as follows from the exact sequence
(
6.1
). Thus, if we prove that
H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}})=0
then it would follow that
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}=0
and

R^{1}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}
is locally free, and our assertion would follow.

Let us consider the morphism of exact sequences on
C_{0}
,

{diagram}

Since the rightmost terms are locally free, the restrictions of these sequences to
C_{x_{0}}
are still exact, so we get
a commutative square of the coboundary maps

{diagram}

We claim that the map
\kappa^{\prime}_{x_{0}}
is injective. Indeed, by assumption,
\kappa_{x_{0}}
is injective.
Also, the map
r
is surjective with the
1
-dimensional kernel spanned by
t\partial_{t}
.
Thus, our claim reduces to the assertion that the restriction of
\kappa^{\prime}_{x_{0}}
to
\ker(r)
is injective.

To prove the last assertion let us consider the embedding
D\subset S
of the formal
1
-dimensional disk with the coordinate
t
, so that
D_{0}=D\cap S_{0}=\{x_{0}\}
.
Then we can identify
\ker(r)
with the image of the natural embedding

{\mathcal{T}}_{D,x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}|_{x_{0}}.

Applying Lemma
6.1
(iv)(v) to the induced family of curves
\pi_{D}:C_{D}\to D
, we get an exact sequence of sheaves on
C_{x_{0}}

0\to{\mathcal{I}}_{q_{x_{0}}}\rTo{t}{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{D},C_{x_{0}}}|_{C_{x_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}}.

where
q_{x_{0}}\in C_{x_{0}}
is the node.
Since
H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{x_{0}}})=H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{I}}_{q_{x_{0}}})=0
, this implies that

H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{D},C_{x_{0}}}|_{C_{x_{0}}})=0.

Therefore, looking at the exact sequence of sheaves on
C_{x_{0}}
,

0\to\omega_{C_{x_{0}}}^{-1}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{C_{D},C_{x_{0}}}|_{C_{x_{0}}}\to(\pi_{D}^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{D,x_{0}})|_{C_{x_{0}}}\to 0

we get that the corresponding coboundary map

H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{D,x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{C_{x_{0}}})\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{D,x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\to H^{1}(C_{x_{0}},\omega_{C_{x_{0}}}^{-1})

(6.8)

in injective.

Now, restricting to
C_{x_{0}}
the natural morphism of exact sequences of vector bundles

{diagram}

and considering the morphism between the corresponding long exact sequences of cohomology on
C_{x_{0}}
,
we deduce that the coboundary map (
6.8
) is equal to the restriction of
\kappa^{\prime}_{x_{0}}
to
\ker(r)
,
which proves our claim.

Finally, by stability of
C_{x_{0}}
, we have
H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},\omega_{C_{x_{0}}}^{-1})=0
.
Hence, the long exact sequence

0\to H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},\omega_{C_{x_{0}}}^{-1})\to H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}})\to{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\rTo{\kappa^{\prime}_{x_{0}}}H^{1}(C_{x_{0}},\omega_{C_{x_{0}}}^{-1})

shows the vanishing of

H^{0}(C_{x_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{C,C_{0}}|_{C_{x_{0}}})
.
∎

Corollary 6.3
.

In the situation of Proposition
6.2
assume in addition that the map from
S
to the moduli space of stable curves induces an isomorphism
on tangent spaces at the origin.
Then the map (
6.6
) is an isomorphism.
Hence, the determinant of the usual Kodaira-Spencer map on
S^{\prime}=S\setminus S_{0}
,

\det KS:\operatorname{Det}{\mathcal{T}}_{S^{\prime}}\to\operatorname{Det}R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-1}_{C/S}|_{S^{\prime}}

is of the form
u/t
, where
u
is a unit (with respect to trivializations regular on
S
).

Proof.

For the second statement, we can argue formally locally. Then we observe that
{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
has a basis
t\partial_{t},\partial_{t_{1}},\ldots,\partial_{t_{d}}
, where
(t,t_{1},\ldots,t_{d})
are
formal coordinates on
S
. Hence the determinant of the natural morphism

{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
with respect to the standard bases is equal to
t
.
∎

6.2.
Super case, NS node

First, let us review the Kodaira-Spencer map for a family of smooth supercurves
\pi:X\to S
.
We have the standard exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{X}\rTo{d\pi}\pi^{*}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}\to 0.

We also have a relative distribution
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}
.
Let us set

{\mathcal{A}}_{X}:=\{v\in{\mathcal{T}}_{X}\ |\ [v,{\mathcal{D}}]\subset{\mathcal{D}}\}.

Then there is an exact sequence (see
[
26
, Sec. 2]
)

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}\to 0

(6.9)

where

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}:={\mathcal{A}}_{X}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}.

Indeed, let
D
be a local generator of
{\mathcal{D}}
. Since
{\mathcal{D}}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}
,
the condition
[v,D](f)=0
, for
f\in\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
, is equivalent to
D(v(f))=0
, i.e., to
v(f)\in\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
.
Furthermore, locally any
v\in\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
can be extended to a section of
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
.
Also, as we have seen before,

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}/{\mathcal{D}}\simeq{\mathcal{D}}^{2}\simeq\omega_{X/S}^{-2}.

Now the Kodaira-Spencer map is the coboundary map

KS:{\mathcal{T}}_{S}\to\pi_{*}\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\simeq R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega_{X/S}^{-2}.

(6.10)

We want to study the behavior of this map near the component of the boundary divisor of the moduli space where a supercurve acquires a NS node.
So let us consider a family of stable supercurves
\pi:X\to S
over a smooth affine base with a smooth map
t:S\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.
We denote by
S_{0}\subset S
the divisor
t=0
and by
\pi_{0}:X_{0}\to S_{0}
the induced family.
We assume that there is a section
q:S_{0}\to X_{0}
such that
q(S_{0})
is the relative node of
X_{0}
and the map
X\to S
is smooth away from
q(S_{0})
.
Furthermore, we assume that the structure sheaf of
X
completed along
q(S_{0})
is generated over
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
by even generators
z_{1},z_{2}
and
odd generators
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
, subject to the relations

z_{1}z_{2}=-t^{2},\ \ z_{1}\theta_{2}=t\theta_{1},\ \ z_{2}\theta_{1}=-t\theta_{2},\ \ \theta_{1}\theta_{2}=0

(6.11)

(so that
q(S_{0})
corresponds to
z_{1}=z_{2}=0
,
\theta_{1}=\theta_{2}=0
).
In this case, by Lemma
3.5
, arguing étale locally, we can assume that
the complement to the node is covered by two charts
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
where
z_{i}
is invertible on
U_{i}
,
and there exist odd sections
s_{i}
(resp., even section
s_{0}
) of
\omega_{X/S}

such that

\begin{array}[]{l}s_{1}=\begin{cases}[dz_{1}|d\theta_{1}]&\text{ on }U_{1},\\
-\frac{t}{z_{2}}[dz_{2}|d\theta_{2}]&\text{ on }U_{2};\end{cases},\\
s_{2}=\begin{cases}\frac{t}{z_{1}}[dz_{1}|d\theta_{1}]&\text{ on }U_{1},\\
[dz_{2}|d\theta_{2}]&\text{ on }U_{2};\end{cases},\\
s_{0}=\begin{cases}\frac{\theta_{1}}{z_{1}}[dz_{1}|d\theta_{1}]&\text{ on }U_{1},\\
-\frac{\theta_{2}}{z_{2}}[dz_{2}|d\theta_{2}]&\text{ on }U_{2};\end{cases}\end{array}

(6.12)

(see
[
13
, Sec. 2.3]
).
Note that the relative derivation
\delta:{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\to\omega_{X/S}
satisfies

\delta(z_{i})=\theta_{i}s_{i},\ \ \delta(\theta_{i})=s_{i},

for
i=1,2
.

Lemma 6.4
.

In the above situation, in the formal neighborhood of the node,
\omega_{X/S}
is generated as an
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module
by global sections
s_{1},s_{2}
and
s_{0}
subject to defining relations

z_{1}s_{2}=ts_{1},\ \ z_{2}s_{1}=-ts_{2},\ \ \theta_{1}s_{2}=\theta_{2}s_{1}=ts_{0},\ \ z_{1}s_{0}=\theta_{1}s_{1},\ \ z_{2}s_{0}=-\theta_{2}s_{2},\ \ \theta_{1}s_{0}=\theta_{2}s_{0}=0.

It has a topological basis over
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
,

z_{1}^{n}s_{1},\ \ z_{2}^{n}s_{2},\ \ z_{1}^{n}\theta_{1}s_{1},\ \ z_{2}^{n}\theta_{2}s_{2},\ \ s_{0},

where
n\geq 0
.

Proof.

It is enough to check this when
S=\C[t]
,
in which case this is discussed in
[
13
, Sec. 2.3]
.
In more detail,
X
is split, with
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}={\mathcal{O}}_{C}\oplus L
, where
C
is the curve
z_{1}z_{2}=-t^{2}
, and
L
is the
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
-module
generated by
\theta_{1}
,
\theta_{2}
(which is a maximal CM-module over
{\mathcal{O}}_{C}
). Then
\omega_{X}=\omega_{C}\oplus\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(L,\omega_{C})
,
and we have an isomorphism
\delta:L\rTo{\sim}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(L,\omega_{C})
.
The section
s_{0}
corresponds to the standard generator of
\omega_{C}
, while
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are the images of the generators
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
of
L

under
\delta
. Another way to prove this is to use Lemma
2.9
.
∎

Note that the line bundle
\omega_{X/S}^{2}:=j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{2}
is generated near the node by the section

e=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{z_{1}}[dz_{1}|d\theta_{1}]^{2}&\text{ on }U_{1},\\
\frac{1}{z_{2}}[dz_{2}|d\theta_{2}]^{2}&\text{ on }U_{2}.\end{cases}

(6.13)

As before, we denote by
{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
the sheaf of derivations of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

and consider the subsheaf
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
consisting of
v
such that
[v,{\mathcal{D}}]\subset{\mathcal{D}}
on the smooth locus
of
\pi:X\to S
. We also set
{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}={\mathcal{A}}_{X}\cap{\mathcal{T}}_{X/S}
. We use a similar definition for
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}
.

Now similarly to the nodal even case we consider the subsheaf

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\subset{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
consisting of
v
such that
v(t)\in(t)
. We have a natural projection

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}.

Note that some of the above sheaves do not have
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module, only the
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-module structure.
Nevertheless, for a subscheme
S^{\prime}\subset S
we have a natural operation of restriction to
\pi^{-1}(S^{\prime})
: we set for an
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-module
{\mathcal{F}}
,

{\mathcal{F}}|_{\pi^{-1}(S^{\prime})}:={\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}}\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{\prime}}.

Below we will prove an analog of Lemma
6.1
.
Note that the slight difference from the case of the usual curves is that the sheaves
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}

are not
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-submodules of
{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
. However, as we know from Theorem
4.4
, there is an isomorphism
of
\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-modules

{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\simeq\omega_{X/S}^{-2}:=j_{*}\omega_{U/S}^{-2},

where
j:U=X\setminus\{q\}\to X
denotes the open embedding and
\omega_{X/S}^{-2}
is a line bundle on
X
(see Theorem
4.1
).

Lemma 6.5
.

(i) There are exact sequences

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to 0

(6.14)

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}\to\pi_{0}^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}\to 0

(6.15)

(ii) In the formal neighborhood of a node, derivations in
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
are in bijection with pairs of functions
a_{1},a_{2}

in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
such that
a_{i}\in(z_{i})
: the corresponding derivation
v
is given by

v(z_{i})=a_{i}+(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})\theta_{i},\ \ v(\theta_{i})=(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})

(6.16)

for
i=1,2
, where
D_{i}=\partial_{\theta_{i}}+\theta_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}
.

For the closed point
s_{0}\in S_{0}
, the natural morphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}/k}

is an isomorphism.

(iii) One has

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}={\mathcal{A}}_{X}

and the natural morphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}/t{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}

is injective.

Proof.

(i) In both cases it is enough to prove surjectivity of the last arrow in the formal neighborhood of
q
.
Let us first check this for
{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}
.
It is enough to extend
t\partial_{t}
to an even derivation
v
in the formal neighborhood of
q
in
X
,
such that
v|_{U_{i}}
preserves
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\cdot(\partial_{\theta_{i}}+\theta_{i}\partial_{z_{i}})
, for
i=1,2
. For this, we can take
v
given by

v(z_{i})=z_{i},\ \ v(\theta_{i})=\frac{1}{2}\theta_{i},\ \ v(t)=t.

This shows that sequence (
6.14
) is exact.

The composed arrow

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to i_{*}\pi_{0}^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S_{0}}

is still surjective and factors through
i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
. This implies exactness
of (
6.15
).

(ii) Recall that
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
is the quotient
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{S_{0}}[\![z_{1},z_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2}]\!]
by the relations

z_{1}z_{2}=0,\ \ z_{1}\theta_{2}=0,\ \ z_{2}\theta_{1}=0,\ \ \theta_{1}\theta_{2}=0.

Thus, a derivation
v
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
is described by the functions

v(z_{1})
,
v(z_{2})
,
v(\theta_{1})
and
v(\theta_{2})
. Furthermore, there should exist
a pair of functions
a_{1}(z_{1},\theta_{1})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{1}^{-1}]
,
a_{2}(z_{2},\theta_{2})\in{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{2}^{-1}]
of the same parity as
v
such that

v(z_{i})=a_{i}+(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})\theta_{i},\ \ v(\theta_{i})=(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})\ \text{ in }{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}],

for
i=1,2
, where
D_{i}=\partial_{\theta_{i}}+\theta_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}
.

The condition
v(z_{1}z_{2})=0
implies that

v(z_{i})\in(z_{i},\theta_{i})\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}

for
i=1,2
. Hence,
a_{i}
is the image of the element
v(z_{i})-v(\theta_{i})\theta_{i}\in(z_{i},\theta_{i})\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}

under the map
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}]
.
Note that the restriction of the latter map to

(z_{i},\theta_{i})={\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{i}]z_{i}\oplus{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{i}]\theta_{i}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}

is an embedding. Thus,

v(z_{i})
is determined by its image in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}]
and the above formulas for
v(z_{i})
hold in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}
with some

a_{i}=f_{i}(z_{i})z_{i}+g_{i}(z_{i})\theta_{i},

for
i=1,2
, with
f_{i},g_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{i}]
.

It follows also that
v(z_{1})\theta_{2}=v(z_{2})\theta_{1}=0
.
Hence, the conditions
v(z_{1}\theta_{2})=v(z_{2}\theta_{1})=0
are equivalent to
v(\theta_{i})\in(z_{i},\theta_{i})
, for
i=1,2
.
Since
D_{i}(a_{i})\equiv g_{i}(z_{i})\mod(\theta_{i})
, this is equivalent to
g_{i}(z_{i})\in(z_{i})
.
The condition
v(\theta_{1}\theta_{2})
is then automatically satisfied.
Note also that the condition
v(\theta_{i})\in(z_{i},\theta_{i})
implies that
v(\theta_{i})
is determined by its image in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}]
.
Thus, the condition on
v
to define a section of
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
is that

a_{i}\in(z_{i})
, for
i=1,2
, which is equivalent to our assertion.

For the last assertion, we note that it is enough to check it in the formal neighborhood of the node. Then the statement follows immediately from
the above explicit description of
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
and from a similar description of
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}/k}
.

(iii) We claim that in fact any derivation
v
in
{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
satisfies
v(t)\in(t,z_{1},z_{2},\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
.
Indeed, we have

v(z_{1})z_{2}+z_{1}v(z_{2})=-2tv(t),

so
tv(t)\in(z_{1},z_{2})
.
Let us write
tv(t)=z_{1}f+z_{2}f^{\prime}
, and decompose
f
and
f^{\prime}
with respect to the topological
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-basis

1,(z_{1}^{n})_{n\geq 1},(z_{2}^{n})_{n\geq 1},(z_{1}^{n}\theta_{1})_{n\geq 0},(z_{2}^{n}\theta_{2})_{n\geq 0}.

Then we get the equation of the form

\displaystyle tv(t)=z_{1}[a_{0}+\sum_{n\geq 1}a_{n}z_{1}^{n}+\sum_{n\geq 1}b_{n}z_{2}^{n}+\sum_{n\geq 0}c_{n}z_{1}^{n}\theta_{1}+\sum_{n\geq 0}d_{n}z_{2}^{n}\theta_{2}]

\displaystyle+z_{2}[a^{\prime}_{0}+\sum_{n\geq 1}a^{\prime}_{n}z_{1}^{n}+\sum_{n\geq 1}b^{\prime}_{n}z_{2}^{n}+\sum_{n\geq 0}c^{\prime}_{n}z_{1}^{n}\theta_{1}+\sum_{n\geq 0}d^{\prime}_{n}z_{2}^{n}\theta_{2}]

The relations imply that the free term of the right-hand side is
-(b_{1}+a^{\prime}_{1})t^{2}
, hence the free term of
v(t)
is divisible by
t
, as claimed.

It follows that for any
v\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
, we have
v(t)\in t\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
, so
v\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}
.
For the last assertion we observe that the kernel of
the restriction map

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}

consists of derivations
v
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
that are in
t\cdot{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
. Hence, this kernel is
t{\mathcal{A}}_{X}=t{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}
.
∎

Now, as in the even case, we consider the
Kodaira-Spencer maps

associated with sequence (
6.14
)

KS:{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-2}_{X/S},

(6.17)

Let
s_{0}\in S_{0}
denote the origin, and consider the restriction of the exact sequence (
6.15
)
to
X_{s_{0}}
, which can be identified with

0\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to T_{s_{0}}S_{0}\otimes\underline{\C}_{X_{s_{0}}}\to 0.

We have the corresponding coboundary map

\kappa_{s_{0}}:T_{s_{0}}S_{0}\to H^{1}(X_{s_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}}).

Now we can prove the following analog of Proposition
6.2
.

Proposition 6.6
.

Assume that the map
\kappa_{x_{0}}
is injective.
Then
R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-2}_{X/S}
is a vector bundle on
S

and the map (
6.17
) is an embedding of a subbundle.

Proof.

First, let
(C_{s_{0}},L_{s_{0}})
be the usual stable curve with a spin structure, underlying
X_{s_{0}}
.
We observe that

\omega^{-2}_{X/S}|_{X_{s_{0}}}=\omega_{C_{s_{0}}}^{-1}\oplus\omega_{C_{s_{0}}}^{-1}\otimes L_{s_{0}}.

Indeed, this follows easily from Theorem
4.1
applied to
the induced family of stable supercurves
X_{D}\to D
over the formal disk
D\subset S_{0}
with the coordinate
t
.
By stability of
C_{s_{0}}
, we get

H^{0}(C_{s_{0}},\omega^{-1})=H^{0}(C_{s_{0}},\omega^{-1}\otimes L_{s_{0}})=0.

In other words, we get the vanishing

H^{0}(X_{s_{0}},\omega^{-2}_{X/S}|_{X_{s_{0}}})=0.

(6.18)

It follows that

\pi_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}=\pi_{*}\omega^{-2}_{X/S}=0,

and that
R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega^{-2}_{X/S}
is a vector bundle on
S
.

Thus,
KS
is a map of vector bundles on
S
, so it is enough to show that the corresponding map of fibers at
s_{0}
,

KS(s_{0}):{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}|_{s_{0}}\to(R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega_{X/S}^{-2})|_{s_{0}}\simeq H^{1}(X_{s_{0}},\omega_{X/S}^{-2}|_{X_{s_{0}}}),

is injective.

To this end, let us consider the morphism of exact sequences on
X_{0}
,

{diagram}

Since the rightmost terms are locally free, the restrictions of these sequences to
X_{s_{0}}
are still exact, so we get
a commutative square of the coboundary maps

{diagram}

Since
\kappa_{s_{0}}
is injective, arguing as before, we see that it is enough to check
that the restriction of
KS(s_{0})
to the
1
-dimensional subspace

{\mathcal{T}}_{D,s_{0}}|_{s_{0}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}|_{s_{0}}

is injective.

Considering the induced family
\pi_{D}:X_{D}\to D
and applying Lemma
6.5
(iv), we get
an inclusion of sheaves on
X_{x_{0}}
,

{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{D},X_{s_{0}}}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}}.

We know that
H^{0}(X_{s_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}})=0
(see Prop.
3.11
(ii)), so we get that

H^{0}(X_{s_{0}},{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{D},X_{s_{0}}}|_{X_{s_{0}}})=0.

Next, restricting the sequence (
6.14
) for the family
X_{D}/D
to
X_{s_{0}}
, we get an exact sequence

0\to\omega_{X_{D}}^{-2}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{D},X_{s_{0}}}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to(\pi_{D}^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{D,s_{0}})|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to 0.

From the above vanishing we get that the corresponding coboundary map

H^{0}(X_{s_{0}},{\mathcal{T}}_{D,s_{0}}|_{s_{0}}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{s_{0}}})\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{D,s_{0}}|_{s_{0}}\to H^{1}(X_{s_{0}},\omega_{X_{D}}^{-2}|_{X_{s_{0}}})

(6.19)

is injective.

Now, restricting to
X_{s_{0}}
the natural morphism of exact sequences

{diagram}

and considering the morphism between the corresponding long exact sequences of cohomology on
X_{s_{0}}
,
we deduce that the coboundary map (
6.19
) is equal to the restriction of
KS(s_{0})
to
\ker(r)
,
which proves our claim.
∎

Note that the Berezinian of the natural morphism
{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{S}
is equal to
t
for an appropriate choice of bases.
As in Corollary
6.3
, this leads to the following result.

Corollary 6.7
.

Assume that the map
\kappa_{x_{0}}
is an embedding and the superdimension of
S
is
3g-3|2g-2
.
Then the map (
6.17
) is an isomorphism. Hence, the Berezinian of the usual Kodaira-Spencer map on
S^{\prime}=S\setminus S_{0}
,

\operatorname{ber}(KS):\operatorname{Ber}({\mathcal{T}}_{S^{\prime}})\to\operatorname{Ber}R^{1}\pi_{*}\omega_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{-2}

is of the form
u/t
, where
u
is invertible on
S
(with respect to trivializations regular on
S
).

6.3.
Super case, Ramond node

Now we consider a family
X\to S
over a smooth affine base
S
with the relative Ramond node
q:S_{0}\to X_{0}=\pi^{-1}(S_{0})
over the divisor
S_{0}=(t=0)

(where
t:S\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
is a smooth morphism),
such that
X\setminus q(S_{0})
is smooth over
S
, and
the completion of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
along
q(S_{0})
is generated over
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
by generators
z_{1},z_{2},\theta
subject to the single relation

z_{1}z_{2}=t.

The distribution is generated by
\partial_{\theta}+\theta z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}
over
z_{i}\neq 0
.

Note that in this case the relative dualizing sheaf
\omega_{X/S}
is a line bundle on
X
.

Lemma 6.8
.

(i) The sequences
(
6.14
) and (
6.15
) are still exact.

(ii) In the formal neighborhood of a node, even (resp., odd) derivations in
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
are in bijection with pairs of even (resp., odd) functions

a_{i}=f_{i}(z_{i})+g_{i}(z_{i})\theta\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{i},\theta_{i}]
,
i=1,2
,
such that
g_{1}(0)=g_{2}(0)
.
The corresponding derivation
v
is given by

v(z_{i})=a_{i}z_{i}+(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})z_{i}\theta,\ \text{ for }i=1,2,

v(\theta)=(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}[(-1)^{|v|}(g_{1}+g_{2}-g_{2}(0))+\theta z_{1}\partial_{z_{1}}(f_{1})+\theta z_{2}\partial_{z_{2}}(f_{2})].

where
D_{i}=\partial_{\theta}+\theta z_{i}\partial_{z_{i}}
.

For a closed point
s_{0}\in S_{0}
, the natural morphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}|_{X_{s_{0}}}\to{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{s_{0}}/k}

is an isomorphism.

(iii) One has

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}={\mathcal{A}}_{X}

and the natural morphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}/t{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to i_{*}{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}}

is injective.

Proof.

(i) To show surjectivity of the morphism
{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}\to\pi^{-1}{\mathcal{T}}_{S,S_{0}}

we observe that the derivation
v\in{\mathcal{A}}_{X,X_{0}}
, given by

v(t)=2t,\ \ v(z_{i})=z_{i},\ \ v(\theta)=0,

extends
2t\partial_{t}
.

(ii)
Here is the description of
{\mathcal{A}}_{X_{0}/S_{0}}
.
The condition
v(z_{1})z_{2}+v(z_{2})z_{1}=0
implies that
v(z_{i})\in(z_{i})
, in particular
v(z_{i})
is determined by its image in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}]
.
Also, we should have
a_{i}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{i},z_{i}^{-1},\theta]
such that

v(z_{i})=a_{i}z_{i}+(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})z_{i}\theta,\ \ v(\theta)=(-1)^{|v|}\frac{1}{2}D_{i}(a_{i})\ \text{ in }{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{0}}[z_{i}^{-1}].

Let us write
a_{i}=f_{i}(z_{i})+g_{i}(z_{i})\theta
. Then
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
could be arbitrary elements of
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{1},\theta]
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}[z_{2},\theta]
(of the same parity) such that

g_{1}(0)=g_{2}(0)
.

(iii) The proof is similar to that of Lemma
6.5
(iv):
we use the condition
v(t)\in(z_{1},z_{2})\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
and the
topological
{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
-basis of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

1,(z_{1}^{n})_{n\geq 1},(z_{2}^{n})_{n\geq 1},(z_{1}^{n}\theta)_{n\geq 0},(z_{2}^{n}\theta)_{n\geq 0}.

∎

Using the same arguments as before we derive the following assertion.

Proposition 6.9
.

The statements of Proposition
6.6
and Corollary
6.7
hold
in the case of a Ramond degeneration as well.

6.4.
Kodaira-Spencer in the presence of NS and Ramond punctures

Everything in Sec.
6.2
and
6.3
has an analog in the case of families of stable supercurves with punctures.
Namely, for such a family
(X,P_{\bullet},R_{\bullet})/S
, we should replace the sheaves
{\mathcal{A}}_{X}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{X/S}

with their intersections
{\mathcal{A}}_{X,P,R}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{(X,P,R)/S}

with the subsheaf
{\mathcal{T}}_{X,P,R}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{X}
of derivations preserving all the punctures (i.e., the corresponding ideals in
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
).
This will not change the local picture near the nodes, however, we need to make some changes in the global statements.

Let us assume that we have a family
(X,P,R)
of stable supercurves with punctures over
S
, acquiring a single node over
S_{0}=(t=0)\subset S
,
so that forgetting the punctures we are in the situation of either Sec.
6.2
or Sec.
6.3
.
