{diagram}

where the horizontal arrows are given by Grothendieck-Serre duality.

Proof.

over

,

{\mathcal{B}}(\omega^{-2m}_{X_{B}/B})\rTo{SD_{X_{B}/B}}{\mathcal{B}}(\omega^{2m+1}_{X_{B}/B}).

Recall that for a sufficiently nice morphism

and a perfect complex
F
on
X

SD_{f}:Rf_{*}(F)\rTo{\sim}Rf_{*}(R\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}(F,\omega_{f}[\dim f]))^{\vee}.

Then we claim that there is an isomorphism of the exact triangles induced by the exact sequences (

(9.7)

Now we claim that commutativity of both squares in (

{diagram}

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
\rho:Y\to X
be a morphism of families of stable supercurves over
B
, which is a fiberwise resolution of the node
Q:B\to X
(so it is an isomorphism
away from
X\setminus Q(B)
). For any coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on

which is locally free of rank

over the smooth locus, and any

,

{diagram}

where in the right vertical arrow we use isomorphisms
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\simeq{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})

coming from the exact sequences

0\to L\to L\otimes\rho_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}\to L\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}\to 0

and the trivialization of

Proof.

supported in the smooth locus of

such that the

on

\rho^{*}s
on

.
Thus, for the top horizontal arrow we use resolutions

\displaystyle R\pi_{*}(\rho_{*}{\mathcal{F}}):

\displaystyle R\pi_{*}(L):

\displaystyle R\pi_{*}(\rho_{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes L):

\displaystyle[\pi_{*}(\rho_{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes L(D))\to\pi_{*}(\rho_{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes L(D)|_{D})],

while for the bottom horizontal arrow we use similar resolutions on

that use the section
\rho^{*}s
of

is the projection,

,

.
Note that

and

are isomorphisms, and the assertion follows from the commutativity of the squares

{diagram}

{diagram}

∎

Lemma 9.5
.

The following diagram is commutative up to a sign

\begin{diagram}

(9.8)

Proof.

Note that it is enough to prove the commutativity of this diagram working in
an étale neighborhood
S
of a stable supercurve
X_{s}
with one NS node. We also choose standard presentation in an étale neighborhood of the node
on
X_{s}
, and use constructions of Sec.
8.3
.

Step 1
. Under isomorphism (
9.6
), the trivialization of
N_{B}
induced by the equation
t=0
of the boundary divisor, corresponds to the trivialization of

\Psi_{1}^{-1}\otimes\Psi_{2}^{-1}
given by
\theta_{1}^{-1}\otimes\theta_{2}^{-1}|_{B}
(where we identify
\Psi_{i}
with
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}/{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
).
Below we will use the notation from Sec.
7
.

First, let us consider the canonical section
c
of
\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}[\Omega_{X/S}\to j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}]
(where
j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}
is placed in degree

).
The proof of Proposition

(i) shows that the complex
\pi_{*}[\Omega_{X/S}\rTo{\iota}j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}]
can be represented by a morphism of trivial bundles with
bases
(b_{i})
,
(c_{i})
such that
b_{1},c_{1},b_{4},c_{4}
are even;
b_{2},c_{2},b_{3},c_{3}
are odd; and the differential
\iota
is given by

\iota(b_{1})=t^{2}c_{1},\ \ \iota(b_{i})=tc_{i},\text{ for }i=2,3,4;\ \ \iota(b_{i})=c_{i},\ \text{ for }i>4.

In addition, over
B=S_{0}
, the elements
b_{1},\ldots,b_{4}
(resp.,
c_{1},\ldots,c_{4}
) induce the standard bases of the sheaves

\pi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}
and
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
from Lemma
7.2
.
This shows that the restriction of
c/t
to
B=S_{0}
corresponds to the trivialization of

\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}[\Omega_{X/S}\to j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}]|_{B}\simeq\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\otimes(\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{K}})^{-1}

induced by the standard bases of
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}
and
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
.

Note that in the commutative square

{diagram}

the bottom horizontal arrow and the right vertical arrows are injective.
Hence, the subsheaves
{\mathcal{K}}\subset\Omega_{X_{B}/B}
in Lemma
7.2
and in Lemma
9.1
(iii) are the same.
This also means that we have an exact sequence

0\to\rho_{*}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B}\to j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}|_{X_{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to 0

and hence, an exact sequence

0\to{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\to\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B}(D_{1}+D_{2})|_{D_{1}+D_{2}}\to\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\to 0

(9.9)

where
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
is the cokernel of the right vertical arrow in the above diagram (see Lemma
9.1
(iv)).

Recall that the trivialization of
\pi_{*}{\mathcal{K}}
used in Lemma
9.1
(iii) comes from the filtration of
{\mathcal{K}}
with the subfactors

Q_{*}(\Psi_{1}^{2}\Psi_{2}^{2})
,
Q_{*}(\Psi_{1}^{2}\Psi_{2})
,
Q_{*}(\Psi_{1}\Psi_{2}^{2})
and
Q_{*}(\Psi_{1}\Psi_{2})
. It is easy to check that this filtration coincides with the
filtration coming from the basis (
7.3
) of
{\mathcal{K}}
.
Let us consider the following basis of
\pi_{*}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B}(D_{1}+D_{2})|_{D_{1}+D_{2}}
:

e_{i}:=\frac{dz_{i}-\theta_{i}d\theta_{i}}{z_{i}},\ \ \frac{\theta_{i}dz_{i}}{z_{i}},\ \ \frac{d\theta_{i}}{z_{i}},\ \ \frac{\theta_{i}d\theta_{i}}{z_{i}},\ \ i=1,2.

Note that it is compatible (up to a sign) with the canonical trivialization of
\operatorname{Ber}(\pi_{*}\Omega_{\widetilde{X}/B}(D_{1}+D_{2})|_{D_{1}+D_{2}}
.
The filtration of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
considered in Lemma
9.1
(iv) is compatible with this basis:
the subsheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}
corresponds to the image of
e_{1}
(or equivalently of
e_{2}
); the next subfactor
{\mathcal{O}}_{Q}
is given by the image
of
\theta_{1}d\theta_{1}/z_{1}
(or of
\theta_{2}d\theta_{2}/z_{2}
); and the quotient
Q_{*}(\Psi_{1}^{-1}\oplus\Psi_{2}^{-1}
is given by the image of
(d\theta_{1}/z_{1},d\theta_{2}/z_{2})
.
Hence, the trivialization of
\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
coming from this basis is compatible with the isomorphism

\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq(\Psi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{2}^{-1})^{-1}

and the trivializations of
\Psi_{i}^{-1}
given by the image of
d\theta_{i}/z_{i}
, i.e., by the generator
\theta_{i}
under the identification
\Psi_{i}^{-1}\simeq\pi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{D_{i}}/{\mathcal{O}}_{S}
.

On the other hand, the images in
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
of the basis vectors

e_{1}-e_{2},\frac{\theta_{1}d\theta_{1}}{z_{1}}-\frac{\theta_{2}d\theta_{2}}{z_{2}},\frac{\theta_{1}dz_{1}}{z_{1}},\frac{\theta_{2}dz_{2}}{z_{2}}

are given by
e-f,f,\theta_{1}e,\theta_{2}e
in terms of the basis (
7.4
) of
{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
.
Hence, the trivialization of
\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}
coming from the latter basis coincides up to a sign with its trivialization induced by
the isomorphism

\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}{\mathcal{C}}_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Ber}^{-1}\pi_{*}\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\Psi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{2}^{-1},

coming from the exact sequence (
9.9
), and by the trivialization of
\Psi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{2}^{-1}
given by
\theta_{1}\otimes\theta_{2}
.

Combining all the above steps we see that the trivialization
c/t|_{B}
of
\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}[\Omega_{X/S}\to j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}]|_{B}
coincides up to a sign with the trivialization
coming from the isomorphism

\operatorname{Ber}\pi_{*}[\Omega_{X/S}\to j_{*}\Omega_{U/S}]|_{B}\simeq\Psi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{2}^{-1}

and the trivialization
\theta_{1}\otimes\theta_{2}
of
\Psi_{1}^{-1}\Psi_{2}^{-1}
.

Step 2
. Let

\phi:{\mathcal{B}}(\omega_{X/\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2})\to{\mathcal{B}}(\omega_{X/\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}})^{-2}\otimes{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})^{-1}(\Delta)

(t=0)
of

Our goal in this step is to compute it.

We start by recalling the exact sequence (which depends on a choice of coordinates)

It is easy to see that the restriction of the embedding

Recall that we choose a sufficiently positive effective divisor

Let us set

We can modify the derivation of the isomorphism (

(9.13)

In addition, exact sequence (
9.11
) gives an isomorphism

(9.14)

On the other hand, we have an isomorphism

\alpha^{\operatorname{reg}}=(\frac{\mu_{s_{1}}}{t},\alpha_{E}):{\mathcal{F}}^{\operatorname{reg}}\rTo{\sim}{\mathcal{G}}

of the parts supported on

(9.15)

Now we obtain that
t\phi
is the isomorphism induced by (
9.13

) and (
9.15

(9.28)

Furthermore, the lower sequence can be identified with

{diagram}

9.3.
Ramond boundary components

Now let
\iota:B\to\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}
be a gluing map for the Ramond boundary component, i.e., one of the maps (
7.9
),
restricted to the locus of smooth supercurves. Recall that we have
a smooth map of relative dimension
0|1
,

p:B\to\overline{B}

to a moduli space
\overline{B}
of smooth supercurves of smaller genus, where the universal smooth supercurve over
\overline{B}
is equipped with a pair of Ramond punctures
R_{1},R_{2}
.
Furthermore,
p
has a structure of the principal bundle over the group scheme
\operatorname{Aut}({\mathcal{P}}_{R_{1}})
which is an extension of
\Z/2
by

\Phi_{1}^{-1}
. Thus, we have a natural isomorphism

K_{B}\simeq p^{*}(K_{\overline{B}}\otimes\Phi_{1}).

On the other hand, we have an exact sequence on the universal stable supercurve
X_{B}
over
B
,

0\to{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{B}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{\widetilde{X}}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{R_{1}}\to 0,

where
\widetilde{X}
is induced by the universal curve
\overline{X}
over
\overline{B}
. Taking push-forwards to
B
and considering the Berezinians,
we get

\iota^{*}\operatorname{Ber}_{1}\simeq p^{*}(\operatorname{Ber}^{\overline{B}}_{1}\otimes\Phi_{1}).

Note that since
\Phi_{1}^{2}
is canonically trivial, this leads to an isomorphism

\iota^{*}\operatorname{Ber}_{1}^{5}\simeq p^{*}((\operatorname{Ber}^{\overline{B}}_{1})^{5}\otimes\Phi_{1}).

We can rewrite (
8.11
) near
B
as an isomorphism

K_{\overline{{\mathcal{S}}}}(\Delta)\simeq\operatorname{Ber}_{1}^{5}.

Thus, the restriction to
B
gives an isomorphism

K_{B}\simeq\iota^{*}\operatorname{Ber}_{1}^{5}.

We conjecture that the following diagram is commutative (up to a sign)

{diagram}

Appendix A
Relative ampleness criterion

Recall that for every superscheme
X
let
{\mathcal{N}}_{X}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
denote the (nilpotent) ideal locally generated by odd functions,
and denote by
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
the usual scheme such that

{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{N}}_{X}.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f:X\to S
be a morphism of superschemes, where
S
is purely even. Assume that
f
factors through
a morphism
\phi:X\to{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\vee})
, where
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}^{+}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
is a supervector bundle on
S
.
Then
\phi
is a closed embedding if and only if
\phi|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}
is a closed embedding (that necessarily factors through
{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee})
) and the morphism

f^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{-}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}\to{\mathcal{N}}_{X}/{\mathcal{N}}_{X}^{2}\otimes\phi^{*}{\mathcal{O}}(1)|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}},

(A.1)

induced by
\phi
, is surjective.

Proof.

The “only if” part is clear, so let us prove the “if” part.
Let us set
{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{N}}_{X}
for brevity.
The underlying map of topological spaces for
\phi
is the same as
\phi_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}:X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}\to{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee})
, so
we just need to show the surjectivity of the homomorphism of sheaves of rings

\phi^{\sharp}:{\bigwedge}^{\bullet}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee})}}(p^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{-}(-1))={\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\vee})}\to\phi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X},

where
p:{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}^{\vee})\to S
is the projection.
Both sides have a natural filtration by powers of an ideal: on
\phi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
we take the filtration
(\phi_{*}{\mathcal{N}}^{i})
, while
on the exterior algebra the filtration
{\bigwedge}^{\geq i}(\cdot)
.
Since the above map is compatible with the surjective map

{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee})}\to\phi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}/{\mathcal{N}})=\phi_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}},

we see that
\phi^{\sharp}
is compatible with the filtrations. Hence, it is enough to check surjectivity on the
consecutive quotients, which follows from the surjectivity of the induced map

p^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{-}\to\phi_{*}({\mathcal{N}}/{\mathcal{N}}^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(1)

or equivalently, of (
A.1
).
∎

The following criterion generalizes a similar result for supermanifolds in
[
25
]
.

Let
f_{{\operatorname{bos}}}:X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}\to S_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
be a morphism of usual schemes induced by
f
.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
f:X\to S
be a flat morphism of superschemes.
If a line bundle
L
on
X
is such that
L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}:=L|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}
is strongly relatively ample over
S_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
, then
L
is strongly relatively ample over
S
.

Proof.

First, let us show that for every coherent sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
on
X
, for
n\gg 0
one has
R^{>0}f_{*}({\mathcal{F}}\otimes L^{n})=0
and
the natural map

f^{*}f_{*}({\mathcal{F}}\otimes L^{n})\to{\mathcal{F}}\otimes L^{n}

(A.2)

is surjective. Let us consider the commutative diagram

{diagram}

We know that the above assertion is true with
(X,f)
replaced by
(X_{{\operatorname{bos}}},f_{{\operatorname{bos}}})
.
Since each
{\mathcal{F}}
has a finite filtration with subsequent quotients that are scheme-theoretically supported on
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
,
we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}=i_{*}{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
with
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
a coherent sheaf on
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
. We have

R^{p}f_{*}(i_{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\otimes L^{n})=j_{*}R^{p}f_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n}),

which immediately implies the vanishing of higher direct images. Furthermore,
the restriction of the map (
A.2
) to
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
is the map

i^{*}f^{*}f_{*}i_{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n})=f_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{*}f_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n})\to({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n})

which is surjective. Hence, (
A.2
) is also surjective.

Applying the above statement for
{\mathcal{F}}={\mathcal{O}}_{X}
we see that for
n\gg 0
,

R^{>0}f_{*}(L^{n})=0
and
f^{*}f_{*}(L^{n})\to L^{n}
is surjective. In particular, in this case

f_{*}(L^{n})
is locally free (here we use flatness of
f
) and the map

X\to{\mathbb{P}}(f_{*}(L^{n})^{\vee})

(A.3)

of superschemes over
S
is well defined. We want to check that (
A.3
) is a closed embedding.
Let us consider the cartesian square

{diagram}

with
S_{0}:=S_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
(however,
X_{0}\neq X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
in general). For
n\gg 0
the above assertions also hold for
(X_{0},f_{0},L^{n}_{0})
, where
L_{0}=L|_{X_{0}}
, and
the base change map

f_{*}(L^{n})|_{S_{0}}\to f_{0*}(L_{0}^{n})

is an isomorphism. Thus, the map
X_{0}\to{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0*}(L^{n})^{\vee})
is obtained by the base change
S_{0}\to S
from the map
(
A.3
). Since
S_{0}
is defined by a nilpotent ideal in
S
, it is enough to check that the map

X_{0}\to{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0*}(L^{n})^{\vee})
is a closed embedding for
n\gg 0
.

Thus, we can assume that
S=S_{0}
. Then we have a decomposition of the supervector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}:=f_{*}(L^{n})
into
even and odd components,

{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}^{+}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{-}.

First, we claim that for
n\gg 0
, the map
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}\to{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee})
, corresponding to the surjection

f^{*}f_{*}(L^{n})|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}\to L^{n}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}},

is an embedding. Indeed, for
n\gg 0
, we have a surjection

{\mathcal{E}}=f_{*}(L^{n})\to f_{*}(i_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}\otimes L^{n})=f_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}(L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n}),

which factors through a surjection
{\mathcal{E}}^{+}\to f_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}(L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n})
.
Since
L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
is relatively ample, we get a composition of two closed embeddings

X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Proj}(S^{\bullet}(f_{{\operatorname{bos}}*}(L_{{\operatorname{bos}}}^{n})))\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{P}}(({\mathcal{E}}^{+})^{\vee}),

and our claim follows.

By Lemma
A.1
, it remains to show that the map

f^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{-}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}\to{\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}

is surjective.
We know that for
n\gg 0
, the map

f^{*}f_{*}({\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n})\to{\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n}

is surjective. Hence, its restriction to
X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}
is still surjective.
Since
{\mathcal{N}}_{X}
is generated by odd functions, we have

{\mathcal{N}}_{X}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}=({\mathcal{N}}_{X}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}})^{-}.

Hence, we get the surjectivity of the map

f^{*}(f_{*}({\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n})^{-})|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}\to{\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n}|_{X_{{\operatorname{bos}}}}.

But
f_{*}({\mathcal{N}}_{X}\otimes L^{n})^{-}
is a subsheaf in
f_{*}(L^{n})^{-}={\mathcal{E}}^{-}
, so we are done.
∎
